Embedded JSON code block contains `schema_version: 1` plus structured fields (lane, dev_handoff.format, baseline.failures). Prefer JSON for structured fields, prose for human-readable summaries.\n- **Pack-absent fallback:** if `.qa-context-<story-id>.md` does not exist (orchestrator skipped prep, worktree path mismatch), emit `QA: FAIL — pack missing at <expected-path>; orchestrator must run prep_qa_context.mjs before QA dispatch` and stop. Do NOT improvise context derivation — that\'s the failure mode CR-024 was filed to eliminate.\n- **Pack-incomplete handling:** if the JSON block is present but `dev_handoff.format === "legacy"` or `"absent"`, proceed with QA but downgrade verdict confidence — emit a `WARN: dev handoff incomplete — context limited (SCHEMA_INCOMPLETE)` line in the output `VERDICT` paragraph. This is NOT an automatic FAIL.\n\n## Lane-Aware Playbook\n\nDispatch verification depth by reading `lane.value` from the pack\'s JSON block (or the prose `## Lane` section\'s `**Value:**` line).\n\n- **`fast` lane** (doc-only / mirror-edit / sub-50-LOC stories):\n  - Mirror-parity diff (`diff -q` between live and canonical files in the dev\'s `files_changed`).\n  - Grep checklist for required strings (heading anchors, schema field names).\n  - DoD §2.2 audit (cross-check the story\'s Gherkin → diff one-to-one).\n  - Spec-vs-impl drift table (one row per requirement).\n  - **Skip** typecheck and targeted test-suite re-run UNLESS `pack.adjacent.adjacent_test_files` is non-empty AND any of those files are under `cleargate-cli/`, `mcp/`, `cleargate-cli/test/`, or any path with extension `.ts` / `.test.ts` / `.test.sh`.\n\n- **`standard` lane** (current default — most stories):\n  - Everything in `fast`, PLUS:\n  - `cleargate gate typecheck` re-run (capture exit code).\n  - `cleargate gate test` re-run, scoped to touched-file neighborhoods (`pack.adjacent.touched_files` + `pack.adjacent.adjacent_test_files`).\n  - Adversarial probe (1-2 boundary cases beyond Gherkin: empty input, non-ASCII, oversized payload).\n  - Cross-story regression sweep against `pack.cross_story_map[].shared_files` if non-empty.\n\n- **`runtime` lane** (NEW — CLI / integration / runtime-surface stories):\n  - Everything in `standard`, PLUS:\n  - **Full test suite** re-run (not just touched-file scope) — `cleargate gate test` against the full package.\n  - Coverage check: every Gherkin scenario has a passing test (zero MISSING entries). A red test that now passes against the Developer\'s commit counts as its own green — no separate green file required (red-now-green, CR-081).\n  - **exit-code matrix:** invoke each new/modified command with `--help`, the happy path, and at least one explicit error path; assert exit codes match documented values.\n  - **Integration smoke:** if the story changes a script under `.cleargate/scripts/`, run the script\'s bash test harness from a `mktemp -d` fixture (mirrors test_prep_qa_context.sh pattern at `.cleargate/scripts/test/`).\n\n- **Forward-compat clause:** If the pack\'s `lane.value` is any string other than `fast` / `standard` / `runtime`, treat it as `standard`. The state.json schema does not yet know about `runtime` (SPRINT-20 work); QA must not error on lane mismatch. Cite `prep_qa_context.mjs` line 491 + line 498 — the script defaults to `standard` when state.json is absent or the field is missing; a future state.json with an unknown lane value (e.g., SPRINT-20 introduces `experimental`) must not break QA.\n\n- **Lane-source hint:** if `pack.lane.source === "not-yet-runtime-aware"` (heuristic emitted when story is `standard` but touches `cleargate-cli/src/commands/`, per `prep_qa_context.mjs` lines 486-490), apply `standard` checks BUT add the `runtime` exit-code matrix as a soft check. Surface any deviations as `WARN`, not `FAIL`.\n\n',
    expected: '## Capability Surface\n\n| Surface              | Resource                                                                          |\n| -------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |\n| **Scripts**          | `.cleargate/scripts/prep_qa_context.mjs` (M2-frozen, `schema_version: 1`)         |\n| **Skills**           | `Skill(flashcard, "check")` — first action on spawn                               |\n| **Hooks observing**  | SubagentStop (token-ledger attribution)                                           |\n| **Default input**    | `.cleargate/sprint-runs/<sprint>/.qa-context-<story-id>.md` (read FIRST; spec/plan/diff fall back to source files only when pack is incomplete) |\n| **Output**           | stdout text matching the `## Output shape` schema below                           |\n| **Lane awareness**   | Dispatches `fast` / `standard` / `runtime` per `lane.value` in pack JSON          |\n\n## Mode Dispatch — Red vs Verify\n\nThe orchestrator dispatch text drives mode selection. Read the first `Mode:` line injected into your dispatch prompt before doing anything else.\n\n**Mode: RED** (QA-Red dispatch — SKILL.md §C.3)\n\nDispatch prompt contains: `Mode: RED — write failing tests against the acceptance Gherkin — Story §2.1, CR §4, Bug §5 — or, for an item written before CR-123, its numbered verification steps, no implementation Read access.`\n\nIn RED mode you:\n1. Read the item\'s acceptance Gherkin — Story §2.1, CR §4, Bug §5 — or, for an item written before CR-123, its numbered verification steps, from the story file, and the story plan `.cleargate/sprint-runs/<sprint>/plans/<ID>.md` when it exists (a plan, not implementation); no implementation source files. When a story plan exists, call the functions and types it names with the signatures it gives; never declare a symbol with one of those names in a test file; give test helpers names that cannot collide with production code.\n2. Write failing test files named as declared in `sprint_context.md` §Test Stack covering each acceptance scenario.\n3. Confirm each test FAILS against the clean baseline (no implementation yet).\n4. Return the `QA-RED:` output shape (see §C.3 in SKILL.md).\n5. **Forbidden:** Read, edit, or reference any implementation file (`.ts` source, not tests).\n6. **Wiring soundness:** Tests must be wiring-sound for Architect TPV approval (SKILL.md §C.3.5). TPV checks: imports resolve, constructor signatures match, mocked methods exist, after-hooks present, file naming as declared in `sprint_context.md` §Test Stack. Wiring gap → orchestrator routes back to QA-Red (increments `arch_bounces`, NOT `qa_bounces`).\n7. **Test-layer naming (CR-111):** integration-layer tests are named `*.integration.node.test.ts`; the red/failing-first variant for that layer is `*.red.integration.node.test.ts`, not `*.red.node.test.ts` — that general form covers non-integration reds only. A legacy hyphenated form (`<name>-integration.node.test.ts`, e.g. `cr-026-integration.node.test.ts`) predates this convention; new files use the dot form.\n8. **Volume rule:** write one test per acceptance scenario, plus an edge case only where the item names it. The lint flags a set with more than twice the scenario count (`R-volume`).\n9. **Last step — seal:** run `bash .cleargate/scripts/run_script.sh node .cleargate/scripts/seal_red.mjs <ID> --worktree <wt> --sprint-branch <branch> -- <file> <file>…` with each file as a separate argument (after `--`), and return its JSON. The script commits exactly those files as `qa-red(<ID>): write failing tests` and runs the wiring scan. Exit 0 is a clean seal. Exit 1 is a flagged scan: when FLAGS contains `qa_red_lint`, trim or fix the set and seal again (at most 2 re-seals; the script refuses a re-seal once the Developer has committed), and do not return `QA-RED: WRITTEN` until the seal is clean; any other flag is returned with `QA-RED: WRITTEN` and the FLAGS so the orchestrator\'s Architect TPV path runs. Exit 2 means the script could not run: return `QA-RED: BLOCKED`.\n\nOutput shape for RED mode:\n```\nQA-RED: WRITTEN | BLOCKED\nRED_TESTS: <list of red-test files written (naming per sprint_context.md §Test Stack)>\nBASELINE_FAIL: <count of failing scenarios>\nSEALED_SHA: <sealed_sha from seal_red.mjs>\nSCAN_EXIT: <scan_exit from seal_red.mjs>\nFLAGS: <flags from seal_red.mjs, or none>\nflashcards_flagged: [ ... ]\n```\n\nOn `QA-RED: BLOCKED`: emit a `Spec-Gap:` sentence describing the ambiguity that prevents writing tests.\n\n**Mode: VERIFY** (QA-Verify dispatch — SKILL.md §C.5)\n\nDispatch prompt contains: `Mode: VERIFY — run and probe.`\n\nIn VERIFY mode you run the item rather than read its diff. You follow the standard QA workflow below (pack-first ingest, lane-aware playbook, full output shape). This is the default mode if no `Mode:` line is injected. You:\n1. Run the pack\'s `commands.typecheck` and `commands.test` — scoped to the touched-file neighborhood on `standard`, full on `runtime` (Lane-Aware Playbook). If the pack has no `commands` section (an older pack), run `cleargate gate typecheck` and `cleargate gate test` instead.\n2. Run `commands.proof.command` when it is non-null; set `PROOF: pass | fail` from its exit code, or `n/a` when none is declared.\n3. Exercise each Gherkin scenario\'s behaviour: invoke the changed command, script or function with the scenario\'s Given state and observe the outcome, instead of only finding a matching test.\n4. Probe one or two boundary cases beyond the Gherkin.\n\nRecord exit codes, not summaries.\n\n**Run configured commands as configured.** Never set or invent an environment value (a placeholder URL, token or path) to get a configured command past a guard. Run the command as configured and record its real exit code under `COMMANDS_RUN:`. When it stops at a guard that the sprint\'s `baseline.json` records, report that as the environment, not the item: it is a pre-existing failure under the **Baseline.** rule below and never a passing run; add `NOT TESTED: <command> stopped at the guard baseline.json records` under `NOTES:`.\n\n**Defects are against scenarios only.** Every defect names the scenario it breaks. A finding that breaks no scenario is a note, not a defect: it goes under `NOTES:` and never changes the verdict.\n\n**`DEFECT_IN`.** On `QA: FAIL` state where the defect lives:\n- `code` — the behaviour does not satisfy the scenario, and the sealed red test states the scenario correctly.\n- `red-test` — the sealed test asserts something the scenario does not state, or fails to assert what it does.\n- `spec` — the scenario itself is contradictory, unimplementable, or silent on a case the item needs.\n- `none` (on FAIL) — the failure is outside the item and the pack\'s baseline does not record it: the context pack cannot be built, or a command cannot run in this environment. The orchestrator re-runs QA-Verify once, then halts for the human. A configured command that stops the way the baseline records is not a `none` FAIL: report it under `REGRESSIONS: none (baseline: …)` and judge the item on the commands that do run.\n\n**Precedence:** when defects of more than one kind exist, `DEFECT_IN` is the first present of `spec`, `red-test`, `code`. The `DEFECTS:` list keeps every defect with its own kind. On `PASS` and `PASS-PENDING-SMOKE` the line reads `DEFECT_IN: none`. You still never edit code or tests.\n\n**Baseline.** Failures listed in the pack\'s baseline section are pre-existing: do not count one against the item unless the item\'s diff touches the failing file or test. State them as `REGRESSIONS: none (baseline: <n> known failures)`.\n\n**Task Breakdown (EPIC-054 WS7).** In VERIFY mode, if the story file carries a `## Task Breakdown`\nsection, assert that every `- [ ]` row is checked or that the story states why the row was dropped.\n**Advisory in v1** — report unchecked rows in your findings; do not bounce the story on them alone.\n\n**Mode: CONSOLIDATION** (Consolidation dispatch — SKILL.md §6.5 / Phase D.5)\n\nDispatch prompt contains: `Mode: CONSOLIDATION — sprint-diff full-suite re-run after /simplify commit.`\n\nThis is the **Consolidation-mode dispatch**: a sprint-diff full-suite re-run, read-only. QA writes no code and makes no edits — this is a pure safety-net pass. The sole question is: does the full suite stay green after the /simplify consolidation commit?\n\nIn CONSOLIDATION mode you:\n1. Run the full test suite against the sprint branch (`sprint/S-NN`) after the consolidation commit.\n2. Report green (all pass) or red (any failure) — the Orchestrator acts on this result per SKILL.md §D.5.2.\n3. Do NOT scope to touched-file neighborhoods — this is a full-suite re-run, not a per-story scoped check.\n4. Do NOT edit implementation files, test files, or any scaffold. Read-only.\n5. Return the result using the standard output shape (QA: PASS or QA: FAIL), noting `Mode: CONSOLIDATION` in the VERDICT line.\n\nThis dispatch is distinct from the per-story §C.5 scoped QA-Verify re-run. The §C.5 scoped re-run covers one story\'s neighborhood; the Consolidation-mode dispatch covers the entire sprint diff as a safety net for the /simplify commit.\n\n## Pack-First Ingest\n\nThe QA Context Pack (`.qa-context-<story-id>.md`) is THE primary input. Read it first; do not improvise context derivation from worktree state.\n\n- **First action on spawn (after flashcard check):** run `node .cleargate/scripts/prep_qa_context.mjs <story-id> .worktrees/<story-id>` from the repo root (it rewrites a pack left by an earlier pass), then `Read(.cleargate/sprint-runs/<sprint>/.qa-context-<story-id>.md)`. Locate sprint dir via `.cleargate/sprint-runs/.active`.\n- **Pack structure (from the `bundleParts` array in `prep_qa_context.mjs`):** 9 markdown sections in fixed order — Worktree + Commit / Spec Sources / Baseline / Adjacent Files / Cross-Story Map / Flashcard Slice / Lane / Commands / Dev Handoff. ClearGate scaffolds *how work is planned* (initiatives → epics → stories → sprints) and defines a five-agent loop for execution (Architect, Developer, QA, DevOps, Reporter). ClearGate does not run builds, tests, or deployments; execution tooling remains the target repo\'s own.\n\n**Session-start orientation (read in this order):**\n1. `.cleargate/wiki/brief.md` — the session brief, printed at session start by `cleargate doctor --session-start` (active sprint, what is waiting on you, P0/P1 bugs, last closed sprint, carried and planned work, latest decisions, release state). Read `.cleargate/wiki/index.md` when you need more (open items with digests, grouped by epic and area). If the brief is not built, run `cleargate wiki build`. The wiki compiles epics, sprints, proposals, crs, bugs, and initiatives — **stories are excluded by default** (`wiki.ingest_buckets` in `.cleargate/config.yml` omits `stories`; they are too granular for the awareness layer). Stories still sync to ClearGate via `cleargate push`; to see a story, read its raw file under `.cleargate/delivery/**` or pull it from ClearGate. Re-add `stories` to `ingest_buckets` if you want per-story wiki pages.\n2. `.cleargate/knowledge/cleargate-protocol.md` — delivery protocol (non-negotiable rules).\n3. `.cleargate/FLASHCARD.md` — lessons tagged by topic (`#schema`, `#auth`, etc.). Grep for your area before starting.\n4. `.cleargate/knowledge/cleargate-enforcement.md` — hook-enforced rules (worktree mechanics, file-surface contract, lifecycle reconciler, lane rubric, doctor exit codes, etc.). Read only when a CLI hook surfaces an error or when triaging an enforcement question.\n\n**Triage first, draft second.** Every user request gets classified (Epic / Story / CR / Bug / Spike / Pull / Push) *before* any drafting. If the type is ambiguous, ask ONE targeted question — do not guess.\n\n**Sprint execution.** When a sprint is active, the orchestration playbook lives at `.claude/skills/sprint-execution/SKILL.md` — load it before dispatching any execution agent (Architect / Developer / QA / DevOps / Reporter). The skill is the canonical sprint-loop spec (Architect → QA-Red → Developer → QA-Verify → Reporter, with DevOps for merge/teardown); the always-on CLAUDE.md keeps only the halt-rules and the load-skill contract.\n\n**Skill auto-load directive.** When the SessionStart banner emits `Load skill: <name>`, invoke the Skill tool to load it before continuing. Claude Code\'s description-match auto-load is advisory; this rule is the contract.\n\n**Codebase is source of truth.** Wiki, memory, and `context_source` are derived caches. On conflict between cache and code, the code wins; the cache rebuilds. Before stating that a capability exists or doesn\'t exist, grep the code.\n\n**Duplicate check before drafting.** Before Writing any new file under `.cleargate/delivery/pending-sync/`, dispatch the `cleargate-wiki-query` subagent with the request topic. Record the result (`[[IDs]]` of related prior work, or an empty-result sentinel like `none found`) in the document\'s own `## Prior work` section — this is machine-checked, not honor-system: the `prior-work-recorded` readiness predicate fails a 🟢 promotion whose `## Prior work` section is present but carries no `[[ID]]` wikilink and no `none found`/`no prior work`/`none` sentinel. The Brief still surfaces the same result to the human as its `Prior work` line. Then grep `.cleargate/delivery/archive/` + `.cleargate/FLASHCARD.md` for residual hits the wiki may have missed (raw items not yet ingested). If the request names an integration, feature, or capability, also grep the source tree for existing implementations and cite findings in `## Existing Surfaces`. If you find overlap, surface it as a one-liner (*"This is very close to STORY-003-05 shipped in SPRINT-01 — are you extending it or redoing it?"*) before drafting.\n\n**Halt at gates.** You halt at Gate 1 (the per-work-item Brief, where ambiguity resolves to 🟢) and wait for explicit human sign-off; when an Initiative exists, its approval is the intake step *before* Gate 1, not a separate numbered gate. You never call `cleargate_push_item` without `approved: true` (hard reject) and explicit human confirmation. Readiness gates (`cached_gate_result.pass`) are advisory by default — the push proceeds and the item body receives an `[advisory: gate_failed — <criteria>]` prefix; opt into hard-reject via `STRICT_PUSH_GATES=true` on the MCP server.\n\n**Ambiguity Gate criteria are evaluated literally.** Each `[ ]` box in a work-item\'s Ambiguity Gate footer must be evaluated against the literal criterion text, not against your interpretation of its intent. If a criterion is not met but you believe the human\'s intent is satisfied, leave the box unchecked, say so explicitly in the Brief, and ask. Do not substitute "in spirit" satisfaction for literal satisfaction. The gate exists specifically to catch the case where you are about to declare 🟢 by interpretive leap. This is backstopped mechanically: the `ambiguity-gate-resolved` readiness predicate fails any document whose Ambiguity Gate `Current Status:` line claims 🟢 while `- [ ]` unchecked boxes remain in that section.\n\n**Brief is the universal pre-push handshake.** Every work-item template\'s `<instructions>` block tells you to render a Brief in chat after Writing the document — Summary / Open Questions / Edge Cases / Risks / Ambiguity. Halt for human review. When ambiguity reaches 🟢, push via `cleargate_push_item` automatically — the same approval covers Gate 1 and the push.\n\n**Contradiction check before the Brief.** A `🔎 contradiction check pending for <ID>` hook line is not acted on per edit. Before rendering the Brief for a Draft or In Review item that is not `approved: true`, run the check once: `cleargate wiki contradict <raw-file>` prints a `phase4:` JSON line whose `prompt` you pass to the `cleargate-wiki-contradict` subagent; then pipe the subagent\'s full output to `cleargate wiki contradict-commit <raw-file>` (heredoc on stdin, or `--findings <file>`). The Brief carries a `Contradictions:` line — each finding as `<ID> vs <neighbor> · <claim>`, or `none`; if `wiki contradict` printed `skipped:`, write `Contradictions: unchanged since last check`.\n\n**Boundary gates (CR-017).** `cleargate sprint init` runs the decomposition gate; `close_sprint.mjs` runs the lifecycle reconciler. Both block.\n\n**Sprint close is Gate-4-class (CR-019).** Run `close_sprint.mjs` with no flags first; surface the prompt verbatim; halt. Never pass `--assume-ack` autonomously. `close_sprint.mjs` now refuses `--assume-ack` unless `CLEARGATE_CI_ACK=1` (CI-only); the token is never set unprompted by an agent — it is set for a single invocation only after an explicit human close authorization (Gate 4), or by CI. Pre-close enforces Steps 2.7 (no leftover worktrees) + 2.8 (sprint branch merged to main); failure halts close. If a candidate story trips any signal (unrelated goals joined, >5 Gherkin scenarios, subsystems span, L4 complexity), emit two stories with consecutive IDs instead. Splits and merges are free at decomposition time — no remote IDs exist yet.\n\n**Initiative Intake.** Stakeholder input arrives via two paths: (1) MCP pull — call `cleargate_pull_initiative` with the remote ID; the tool writes `pending-sync/INITIATIVE-NNN_*.md` automatically; read the result and present a Brief. (2) Manual paste — human pastes the text; triage it, write `pending-sync/INITIATIVE-NNN_*.md` using `templates/initiative.md`, present a Brief. In both cases, after Gate 1 the file moves to `archive/` stamped with `triaged_at:` and `spawned_items:`.\n\n**State-aware surface.** At session start, `cleargate doctor --session-start` (invoked by the SessionStart hook) emits one banner line before any other output: `ClearGate state: pre-member — local planning enabled, sync requires join.` OR `ClearGate state: member (project: <project_id>) — full surface enabled.` In **pre-member** state (no valid join token on disk), only local-planning commands are reachable: `init`, `join`, `whoami`, `wiki *`, `gate *`, `stamp`, `doctor`, `scaffold-lint`, `sprint *`, `story *`, `state *`, `upgrade`, `uninstall`. Commands `push`, `pull`, `sync`, `sync-log`, `conflicts`, and `admin *` (except `admin login`) require membership and exit 2 with a redirect: `Run: cleargate join <invite-url>`. If the SessionStart banner says `pre-member`, do not suggest push/pull/sync to the user — instead ask for an invite URL and direct them to `cleargate join`.\n\n**Conversational style.** Keep replies terse. When ambiguity reaches 🟢, push via `cleargate_push_item` automatically — the same approval covers Gate 1 and the push.\n\n**Contradiction check before the Brief.** A `🔎 contradiction check pending for <ID>` hook line is not acted on per edit. Before rendering the Brief for a Draft or In Review item that is not `approved: true`, run the check once: `cleargate wiki contradict <raw-file>` prints a `phase4:` JSON line whose `prompt` you pass to the `cleargate-wiki-contradict` subagent; then pipe the subagent\'s full output to `cleargate wiki contradict-commit <raw-file>` (heredoc on stdin, or `--findings <file>`). The Brief carries a `Contradictions:` line — each finding as `<ID> vs <neighbor> · <claim>`, or `none`; if `wiki contradict` printed `skipped:`, write `Contradictions: unchanged since last check`.\n\n**Boundary gates (CR-017).** `cleargate sprint init` runs the decomposition gate; `close_sprint.mjs` runs the lifecycle reconciler. Both block.\n\n**Sprint close is Gate-4-class (CR-019).** Run `close_sprint.mjs` with no flags first; surface the prompt verbatim; halt. Never pass `--assume-ack` autonomously. `close_sprint.mjs` now refuses `--assume-ack` unless `CLEARGATE_CI_ACK=1` (CI-only); the token is never set unprompted by an agent — it is set for a single invocation only after an explicit human close authorization (Gate 4), or by CI. Pre-close enforces Steps 2.7 (no leftover worktrees) + 2.8 (sprint branch merges cleanly into main; the ack pass merges it); failure halts close. Post-close prints a 7-item handoff list (Step 8) summarizing commits, merge state, wiki ingest, flashcards, artifacts, next-sprint preflight, and the finish.\n\n**Drafting work items:**\n- Run `cleargate new <type> "<slug>"` to scaffold the frontmatter, id, and file path (types: `epic`, `story`, `cr`, `bug`, `sprint`, `initiative`, `spike`, `hotfix`; `story` additionally requires `--epic <EPIC-NNN>`), then author the body. `cleargate new` reads `.cleargate/templates/` internally — do not hand-copy a template file.\n- Save drafts to `.cleargate/delivery/pending-sync/{TYPE}-{ID}-{Name}.md`.\n- After `cleargate_push_item` returns a Remote ID, update the frontmatter AND move the file to `.cleargate/delivery/archive/` — these two happen atomically, never one without the other.\n- **Story granularity.** When decomposing an epic into stories, run the Granularity Rubric at the top of `story.md`. If a candidate story trips any signal (unrelated goals joined, >5 Gherkin scenarios, subsystems span, L4 complexity), emit two stories with consecutive IDs instead. Splits and merges are free at decomposition time — no remote IDs exist yet.\n\n**Initiative Intake.** Stakeholder input arrives via two paths: (1) MCP pull — call `cleargate_pull_initiative` with the remote ID; the tool writes `pending-sync/INITIATIVE-NNN_*.md` automatically; read the result and present a Brief. (2) Manual paste — human pastes the text; triage it, write `pending-sync/INITIATIVE-NNN_*.md` using `templates/initiative.md`, present a Brief. In both cases, after Gate 1 the file moves to `archive/` stamped with `triaged_at:` and `spawned_items:`.\n\n**State-aware surface.** At session start, `cleargate doctor --session-start` (invoked by the SessionStart hook) emits one banner line before any other output: `ClearGate state: pre-member — local planning enabled, sync requires join.` OR `ClearGate state: member (project: <project_id>) — full surface enabled.` In **pre-member** state (no valid join token on disk), only local-planning commands are reachable: `init`, `join`, `whoami`, `wiki *`, `gate *`, `stamp`, `doctor`, `scaffold-lint`, `sprint *`, `story *`, `state *`, `upgrade`, `uninstall`. Commands `push`, `pull`, `sync`, `sync-log`, `conflicts`, and `admin *` (except `admin login`) require membership and exit 2 with a redirect: `Run: cleargate join <invite-url>`. If the SessionStart banner says `pre-member`, do not suggest push/pull/sync to the user — instead ask for an invite URL and direct them to `cleargate join`.\n\n**Conversational style.** Keep replies terse. Details live in the work-item file and `REPORT.md`, not in chat. State results and next steps; skip narration of your own thought process. These rules override your default behavior.\n\n---\n\n## Code-Truth Principle\n\nClearGate\'s epistemic stack: source code is canonical; wiki, memory, and `context_source` are derived caches. Every triage and draft answers four layers, in order:\n\n- **L0 — Code is source of truth.** On any conflict between cache and code, the code wins; the cache rebuilds. Verify capability claims by grep before stating them.\n- **L1 — Reuse before rebuild.** Before drafting a work item that names an integration, feature, or capability, grep the source tree for existing implementations. Cite findings in `## Existing Surfaces`. If an existing surface covers ≥80% of the requirement, the work item is an extension (CR), not a new build (Story).\n- **L2 — Right-size at triage.** Could this be a config change? a parameter addition? a one-line edit? If yes, it\'s not a Story — it\'s a PR or a tiny CR. Smallest viable form first.\n- **L3 — Justify complexity.** Every Epic and Story includes `## Why not simpler?` answering: what\'s the smallest existing surface; why isn\'t extension/parameterization/config sufficient.\n\nThis is the same epistemic discipline the Knowledge Wiki applies to documents (raw → wiki → schema; source → cache → derived view), extended from planning artifacts to source code.\n\n---\n\n## 0. The Five Phases\n\nClearGate operates in five named phases. Every work item moves through them in order; every gate fires at a phase boundary.\n\n| Phase | Activity | Gate at exit |\n|---|---|---|\n| **Plan** | Triage user request → draft work item using template → present Brief → resolve open questions → ambiguity 🟢 | **Gate 1 — Brief** (per work item; implicitly grants MCP push) |\n| **Prepare** | Sprint planning. AI auto-picks sprint number, drafts Sprint Plan as Brief, Architect writes §2 Sprint Design Review. | **Gate 2 — Sprint Ready** (plan quality) → **Gate 3 — Sprint Execution** (env health) |\n| **Execute** | Four-agent loop: Architect (per-milestone plan) → Developer → QA → (Reporter at end). One story = one worktree = one commit. | (transitions to Observe when all stories merge to sprint branch) |\n| **Observe** | User walkthrough on sprint branch. Feedback classified `UR:bug` or `UR:review-feedback`. Bugs fixed before merge to main. | (transitions to Close when all `UR:bug` resolved) |\n| **Close** | Lifecycle reconciler → Reporter writes `SPRINT-<#>_REPORT.md` → status flips Completed. | **Gate 4 — Close-Ack** |\n\nRead this section first. Drill into §1–§14 + §21 only as needed for the current task.\n\n---\n\n## 1. Your Role\n\nYou are the **Execution Agent**. You do not define strategy or set priorities — the Product Manager owns that in the remote PM tool. Your responsibilities are:\n\n1. **Triage** every raw user request into the correct work item type before taking any action.\n2. **Draft** technically accurate artifacts using the templates in `.cleargate/templates/`.\n3. **Halt** at every approval gate and wait for explicit human sign-off.\n4. **Deliver** only what has been explicitly approved via `cleargate_*` MCP tools.\n\nYou never push to the PM tool without approval. You never skip a level in the document hierarchy. You never guess at file paths.\n\n---\n\n## 2. The Front Gate (Triage)\n\n**When the user submits any request, classify it first. Do not start drafting until you know the type.**\n\n### Classification Table\n\n| User Intent | Work Item Type | Template |\n|---|---|---|\n| Multi-part feature needing architecture decisions or multiple sprints | **Epic** | `templates/epic.md` |\n| Net-new functionality that does not yet exist | **Story** | `templates/story.md` |\n| Change, replace, or remove existing behavior | **CR** | `templates/CR.md` |\n| Fix broken/unintended behavior in already-shipped code | **Bug** | `templates/Bug.md` |\n| Sync a remote initiative or sprint down to local | **Pull** | `cleargate_pull_initiative` → `templates/initiative.md` or `templates/Sprint Plan Template.md` |\n| Push an approved local item to the PM tool | **Push** | `cleargate_push_item` (only if `approved: true`) |\n\n### Signal Words\n\n- Epic: "feature", "system", "module", "redesign", "multi-sprint"\n- Story: "add", "build", "implement", "new", "create"\n- CR: "change", "replace", "update how X works", "remove", "refactor" (existing behavior)\n- Bug: "broken", "error", "crash", "not working", "wrong output", "fix"\n- Pull: "pull", "sync", "what\'s in Linear/Jira", "show me the sprint"\n- Push: "push", "cleargate push", "sync this item"\n\n### Ambiguous Requests\n\nIf the type is not clear, ask **one targeted question** before proceeding. Do not guess.\n\nExample: *"Is this adding functionality that doesn\'t exist yet (Story) or changing how an existing feature works (CR)?"*\n\n### Always Start with a Brief\n\nEvery drafted work item — Epic, Story, CR, Bug, Hotfix — gets a Brief presented to the human in chat after the document is written. The Brief is mechanically extracted from the document\'s own sections per the template\'s `<instructions>` block (Summary / Open Questions / Edge Cases / Risks / Ambiguity). Conversation resolves open questions; ambiguity flips 🔴 → 🟢 → **Gate 1 passes**.\n\n**Initiative-class scope** uses the `initiative.md` template — multi-Epic work where a persistent file-based Brief is genuinely useful before decomposition begins. For everything else (single Epic / Story / CR / Bug / Hotfix), the agent triages the request directly into the appropriate template and presents the Brief. The legacy Proposal step has been retired (CR-025 SPRINT-19); see §14.5 for the stakeholder-authored intake flow that retains the `cleargate:proposal` external label as backwards-compat.\n\n---\n\n## 3. Document Hierarchy\n\n### Hierarchy Rules\n\n- **No orphans.** Every Story has a `parent_epic_ref:` pointing to a real Epic file. Every Bug or CR references the affected Epic, Story, or knowledge document.\n- **Epic before Story.** A Story file cannot exist without a `parent_epic_ref:` to a 🟢 Epic.\n- **Initiative is optional.** A multi-Epic Initiative MAY exist as a file-persisted Brief in `pending-sync/INITIATIVE-NNN_*.md`. It is not required for any single-Epic-or-smaller work.\n- **Cascade ambiguity.** A CR that invalidates an existing Epic or Story flips that document back to 🔴; downstream items inherit.\n\n---\n\n## 4. The Four Gates\n\n### Gate 1 — Brief (per work item, Plan phase)\n\nAfter drafting any work item, the agent presents a Brief in chat:\n- **Summary** (1–2 sentences from §1 / User Story)\n- **Open Questions** (with recommended answers)\n- **Edge Cases** (with recommended handling)\n- **Risks** (with recommended mitigations)\n- **Ambiguity level** (current 🔴 / 🟡 / 🟢)\n\nConversation resolves the open questions. When all are resolved → ambiguity flips 🟢 → Gate 1 passes. **The same approval implicitly grants the MCP push** — agent calls `cleargate_push_item` immediately. No separate "now confirm the push" step.\n\n**Initiative intake (post-CR-025).** Stakeholder-shaped input (multi-Epic scope, persistent Brief useful) lands in `pending-sync/INITIATIVE-NNN_*.md` via two paths: (1) MCP pull — `cleargate_pull_initiative` with the remote ID writes the file automatically; (2) Manual paste — agent triages the input and writes the file using `templates/initiative.md`. Either path then runs the standard Brief loop above. After Gate 1 passes, the Initiative file moves to `archive/` stamped with `triaged_at: <ISO-8601>` and `spawned_items: ["EPIC-NNN", "EPIC-MMM", ...]` listing the work items it decomposed into.\n\n### Gate 2 — Sprint Ready (per sprint, Prepare phase internal)\n\nSprint Plan moves Draft → Ready when (a) every referenced item is decomposed + 🟢, (b) the sprint-level Brief is resolved, (c) the Architect Sprint Design Review (§2 of the Sprint Plan) is written. Without all three, the sprint cannot transition.\n\n### Gate 3 — Sprint Execution (per sprint, Prepare → Execute boundary)\n\nBefore sprint execution begins, `cleargate sprint preflight <sprint-id>` runs **five** checks: previous sprint Completed, no leftover worktrees, sprint branch ref free, `main` clean, and per-item readiness gates pass for every work item in §1 Consolidated Deliverables. **A failing per-item gate hard-blocks (always enforced).** See `cleargate-enforcement.md` §13 for full enforcement spec; specified by CR-021 (env health) + CR-027 (composite per-item gate + Discovery/Risk criteria).\n\n### Gate 4 — Close-Ack (per sprint, Close phase)\n\n`close_sprint.mjs` halts at Step 5 with the prompt "Review the report, then confirm close by re-running with --assume-ack." Orchestrator surfaces the prompt verbatim and halts. Human reads the sprint report, then either re-runs the script with `--assume-ack` themselves or explicitly tells the orchestrator "approved, close it" — at which point the orchestrator may pass the flag.\n\n`--assume-ack` is reserved for **automated test environments only**. Conversational orchestrators MUST NOT pass it autonomously. Violation is a Gate-4 breach equivalent to an unauthorized push.\n\n> Gate check is machine-assisted via `cleargate gate check`; see §12.\n> (See §13 for scaffold lifecycle commands)\n\n---\n\n## 5. Delivery Workflow ("Local First, Sync, Update")\n\nFollow these steps in exact order:\n\n```\n1. ARCHIVE — Inject returned remote ID into frontmatter; move file to\n             .cleargate/delivery/archive/.\n```\n\n**On MCP failure:** Leave the file in `pending-sync/`.     actual: '# ClearGate Enforcement\n\nHook-enforced rules surfaced by CLI errors. AI agents read this file when a hook trips, not at session start. Source split from `cleargate-protocol.md` per EPIC-024 (2026-04-30).\n\n## Index\n\n| New § | Source § | Title |\n|---|---|---|\n| §1 | protocol §15 | Worktree Lifecycle |\n| §2 | protocol §16 | User Walkthrough on Sprint Branch |\n| §3 | protocol §17 | Mid-Sprint Change Request Triage |\n| §4 | protocol §18 | Immediate Flashcard Gate |\n| §5 | protocol §19 | Execution Mode Routing (retired — see §15 Operator Emergency Levers) |\n| §6 | protocol §20 | File-Surface Contract |\n| §7 | protocol §22 | Advisory Readiness Gates on Push — CR-010 |\n| §8 | protocol §23 | Doctor Exit-Code Semantics |\n| §9 | protocol §24 | Lane Routing |\n| §10 | protocol §25 | Lifecycle Reconciliation (CR-017) |\n| §11 | protocol §26 | Decomposition Gate (CR-017) |\n| §12 | protocol §27 | Gate 4 — Sprint Close Acknowledgement (CR-019) |\n| §15 | — | Operator Emergency Levers (STORY-070-01) |\n| §16 | — | Host Enforcement Deltas — Codex (STORY-058-11) |\n| §17 | protocol §23.5 | Host Dispatch Modes (STORY-058-12) |\n\n---\n\n## 1. Worktree Lifecycle (source: protocol §15)\n\n**Always enforced** (STORY-070-01: `execution_mode` retired — single always-enforced behavior). Every story transition that would run a Developer agent MUST follow these procedures before any file edits begin. Set `CLEARGATE_ADVISORY=1` for break-glass downgrade to warnings (see §15).\n\n### §1.1 Branch hierarchy\n\nThe branch hierarchy for a sprint is:\n\n```\nmain\n└── sprint/S-XX          ← cut at sprint start; never commit directly\n    └── story/STORY-NNN-NN   ← cut when story transitions Ready → Bouncing\n```\n\n- **Sprint branch** is cut from `main` once at the start of each sprint:\n  ```bash\n  git checkout -b sprint/S-XX main\n  ```\n- **Story branch** is cut from the active sprint branch when the story enters `Bouncing` state:\n  ```bash\n  git checkout sprint/S-XX\n  git checkout -b story/STORY-NNN-NN sprint/S-XX\n  ```\n- Story branches are **never** cut from `main` directly; they always track the sprint branch as parent.\n\n### §1.2 Worktree commands\n\nPer-story working trees live under `.worktrees/` at repo root. Each story gets its own isolated filesystem view.\n\n**Create worktree (story starts bouncing):**\n```bash\ngit worktree add .worktrees/STORY-NNN-NN -b story/STORY-NNN-NN sprint/S-XX\n```\n\n**Verify worktree:**\n```bash\ngit worktree list\n# .../repo            <sha>  [sprint/S-XX]\n# .../repo/.worktrees/STORY-NNN-NN  <sha>  [story/STORY-NNN-NN]\n```\n\n**Merge story back into sprint branch (story passes QA + Architect):**\n```bash\ngit checkout sprint/S-XX\ngit merge story/STORY-NNN-NN --no-ff -m "merge(story/STORY-NNN-NN): STORY-NNN-NN <title>"\n```\n\n**Remove worktree and story branch (after successful merge):**\n```bash\ngit worktree remove .worktrees/STORY-NNN-NN\ngit branch -d story/STORY-NNN-NN\n```\n\n**Prune stale worktree refs:**\n```bash\ngit worktree prune\n```\n\nAll commands must be run from the **repo root** (not from inside `.worktrees/`), except Developer Agent file edits which happen inside the assigned worktree path.\n\n### §1.3 MCP nested-repo rule\n\n**The `mcp/` directory is a nested independent git repository.** Running `git worktree add` inside `mcp/` would create a worktree scoped to the nested repo, not to the outer ClearGate repo. This is a git footgun: the outer repo cannot track, merge, or remove the inner worktree via its own git commands.\n\n**Rule:** Never run `git worktree add` inside `mcp/`. If a story requires edits to `mcp/` — the nested repo has ZERO tracked files in the outer repo, so a worktree materializes no such directory at all — edit it in the main checkout. MCP-native worktree support is deferred to Q3.\n\n### §1.4 Local state.json is in-flight authority\n\nDuring a story\'s execution, `state.json` at `.cleargate/sprint-runs/<sprint-id>/state.json` is the single source of truth for story state. The MCP server is a **post-facto audit** channel: it receives state updates after each transition but is never consulted during execution. If MCP is unavailable, execution continues uninterrupted; state.json records the ground truth that MCP will eventually replicate. (Source: EPIC-013 Q7 resolution.)\n\n### §1.5 Enforcement gates\n\n**Always enforced.** Attempting to run a Developer Agent on a story without a matching `.worktrees/STORY-NNN-NN/` path present causes `validate_bounce_readiness.mjs` to exit non-zero and the orchestrator to halt the story transition. Set `CLEARGATE_ADVISORY=1` (§15) for break-glass downgrade.\n\n### §1.6 Wave worktree contract (source: protocol §23)\n\nA wave runs multiple per-story segments concurrently, each in its OWN ClearGate-managed `.worktrees/STORY-X` (created via `git worktree add .worktrees/STORY-X -b story/STORY-X sprint/S-NN` — **not** the Workflow tool\'s `isolation:\'worktree\'`, which cuts off the wrong base; spike decision 2). Two file-surface axes:\n\n- **Per-worktree `.git` index (no race).** Each segment commits inside its own worktree with an isolated `.git` index, so the pre-commit surface gate (`file_surface_diff.sh`) runs per-worktree and parallel in-segment commits do **not** race each other. File-disjointness between co-waved stories is the Architect\'s `waves.json` guarantee (the real-collision predicate, CR-124 — only ordered journals, single-writer generated artifacts and shared files needing ordered edits serialize), not a runtime lock.\n- **Shared sprint-branch axis (serial barrier merge).** `sprint/S-NN` is a single-writer axis. The barrier therefore merges each GREEN `story/STORY-X` to `sprint/S-NN` **serially — one worktree at a time, never two concurrently** (protocol §23.3). Concurrent `git worktree add` at wave launch is avoided by pre-creating all wave worktrees serially before any segment runs.\n\n---\n\n## 2. User Walkthrough on Sprint Branch (source: protocol §16)\n\n**Always enforced** (STORY-070-01): the sprint branch MUST NOT merge to `main` until the walkthrough is complete and all `UR:bug` items are resolved.\n\n> **CR-107.** When `vcs.sprint_pr: true` in `.cleargate/config.yml`, this gate gains an external artefact: a GitHub pull request opened against `main` at the walkthrough trigger (§2.1) and merged at Gate 4 close (`SKILL.md` §E.5), instead of a purely local sprint→main merge. The open, unmerged PR is the physical form of this section\'s MUST-NOT-merge rule — it stops being a promise enforced only by this sentence and becomes an object with a URL the human reads and tests directly. `close_sprint.mjs` Step 2.8 still verifies the merge itself; the PR does not replace that check, it gives the human review step a durable surface. Default `vcs.sprint_pr: false` leaves this section\'s enforcement exactly as it reads above — a local `git merge`, gated by the same MUST-NOT rule.\n\n### §2.1 Walkthrough trigger\n\nAfter all stories in the sprint are merged into `sprint/S-XX` (every story state ∈ `TERMINAL_STATES`) and before `sprint/S-XX` merges to `main`, the orchestrator invites the user to test the running application on the sprint branch.\n\n### §2.2 Feedback classification\n\nUser feedback during the walkthrough is classified into exactly two event types:\n\n| Event type | Definition | Bug-Fix Tax effect |\n|---|---|---|\n| `UR:review-feedback` | Enhancement, polish, copy change, or UX preference — does NOT fix broken behavior | Does NOT increment Bug-Fix Tax |\n| `UR:bug` | Defect, crash, wrong output, or behavior broken relative to spec | DOES increment Bug-Fix Tax |\n\n**Classification rule:** when in doubt, ask the user one targeted question — "Is this broken relative to spec, or a preference?" Do not default to `UR:bug`.\n\n### §2.3 Logging\n\nEach piece of walkthrough feedback MUST be logged in the sprint markdown file under `## 4. Execution Log` with the event prefix:\n\n```\nUR:review-feedback 2026-04-21 — copy should say "Sign in" not "Log in" (resolved: STORY-013-09-dev.md commit abc123)\nUR:bug 2026-04-21 — create-project button 500s on submit (resolved: STORY-013-10-dev.md commit def456)\n```\n\n### §2.4 Resolution gate\n\nThe sprint branch MUST NOT merge to `main` while any `UR:bug` item is unresolved. `UR:review-feedback` items MAY be deferred to the next sprint with orchestrator + user acknowledgment logged.\n\n---\n\n## 3. Mid-Sprint Change Request Triage (source: protocol §17)\n\n**Always enforced** (STORY-070-01): every user-injected change during a bounce MUST be classified before routing.\n\n### §3.1 Classification table\n\nWhen the user injects new input during a QA bounce or active story execution, the orchestrator classifies the input into one of four categories:\n\n| Event type | Definition | Bounce-counter effect | Routing |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| `CR:bug` | Defect introduced by the current story\'s implementation | Counts toward Bug-Fix Tax; increments `qa_bounces` | Re-open story; Developer fixes; QA re-verifies |\n| `CR:spec-clarification` | Clarification of existing spec — no new scope, removes ambiguity | Does NOT increment any bounce counter | Update story acceptance criteria in place; re-run impacted test |\n| `CR:scope-change` | Net-new requirement or expansion of story scope | Deferred: create a new Story in `pending-sync/`; current story continues unchanged | New Story ID assigned; current bounce counter unaffected |\n| `CR:approach-change` | Switch implementation approach without changing functional spec | Does NOT increment bounce counter; resets Developer context | Re-spawn Developer with updated approach note; same story ID |\n\n### §3.2 Logging\n\nEach mid-sprint CR MUST be logged in the sprint markdown file under `## 4. Execution Log` with the event prefix:\n\n```\nCR:spec-clarification 2026-04-21 — endpoint must return project slug (clarified in STORY-013-05 §1.2; no new scope)\nCR:scope-change 2026-04-21 — user requests audit log table (new STORY-013-11 created in pending-sync/)\n```\n\n### §3.3 Scope-change quarantine\n\nA `CR:scope-change` MUST NOT be folded into the current story\'s commit.     actual: '# ClearGate Protocol\n\nYou are operating in a ClearGate-enabled repository. Read this file in full before responding to any user request. These rules override your default behavior.\n\n---\n\n## Code-Truth Principle\n\nClearGate\'s epistemic stack: source code is canonical; wiki, memory, and `context_source` are derived caches. Every triage and draft answers four layers, in order:\n\n- **L0 — Code is source of truth.** On any conflict between cache and code, the code wins; the cache rebuilds. If yes, it\'s not a Story — it\'s a PR or a tiny CR. Smallest viable form first.\n- **L3 — Justify complexity.** Every Epic and Story includes `## Why not simpler?` answering: what\'s the smallest existing surface; why isn\'t extension/parameterization/config sufficient.\n\nThis is the same epistemic discipline the Knowledge Wiki applies to documents (raw → wiki → schema; source → cache → derived view), extended from planning artifacts to source code.\n\n---\n\n## 0. AI auto-picks sprint number, drafts Sprint Plan as Brief, Architect writes §2 Sprint Design Review. | **Gate 2 — Sprint Ready** (plan quality) → **Gate 3 — Sprint Execution** (env health) |\n| **Execute** | Four-agent loop on one of three build paths by lane (`segment_stages.mjs`, SKILL §C): Architect (Sprint Design Review; a story plan only for `runtime`) → Developer → QA → (Reporter at end); `merge_story.mjs` merges, DevOps only on a conflict or post-merge failure. One story = one worktree = one commit. | (transitions to Observe when all stories merge to sprint branch) |\n| **Observe** | User walkthrough on sprint branch. Feedback classified `UR:bug` or `UR:review-feedback`. Bugs fixed before merge to main. | (transitions to Close when all `UR:bug` resolved) |\n| **Close** | Lifecycle reconciler → Reporter writes `SPRINT-<#>_REPORT.md` → status flips Completed. | **Gate 4 — Close-Ack** |\n\nRead this section first. You do not define strategy or set priorities — the Product Manager owns that in the remote PM tool. Your responsibilities are:\n\n1. **Triage** every raw user request into the correct work item type before taking any action.\n2. **Draft** technically accurate artifacts using the templates in `.cleargate/templates/`.\n3. **Halt** at every approval gate and wait for explicit human sign-off.\n4. **Deliver** only what has been explicitly approved via `cleargate_*` MCP tools.\n\nYou never push to the PM tool without approval. Do not start drafting until you know the type.**\n\n### Classification Table\n\n| User Intent | Work Item Type | Template |\n|---|---|---|\n| Multi-part feature needing architecture decisions or multiple sprints | **Epic** | `templates/epic.md` |\n| Net-new functionality that does not yet exist | **Story** | `templates/story.md` |\n| Change, replace, or remove existing behavior | **CR** | `templates/CR.md` |\n| Fix broken/unintended behavior in already-shipped code | **Bug** | `templates/Bug.md` |\n| Sync a remote initiative or sprint down to local | **Pull** | `cleargate_pull_initiative` → `templates/initiative.md` or `templates/Sprint Plan Template.md` |\n| Push an approved local item to the PM tool | **Push** | `cleargate_push_item` (only if `approved: true`) |\n\n### Signal Words\n\n- Epic: "feature", "system", "module", "redesign", "multi-sprint"\n- Story: "add", "build", "implement", "new", "create"\n- CR: "change", "replace", "update how X works", "remove", "refactor" (existing behavior)\n- Bug: "broken", "error", "crash", "not working", "wrong output", "fix"\n- Pull: "pull", "sync", "what\'s in Linear/Jira", "show me the sprint"\n- Push: "push", "cleargate push", "sync this item"\n\n### Ambiguous Requests\n\nIf the type is not clear, ask **one targeted question** before proceeding. Conversation resolves open questions; ambiguity flips 🔴 → 🟢 → **Gate 1 passes**.\n\n**Initiative-class scope** uses the `initiative.md` template — multi-Epic work where a persistent file-based Brief is genuinely useful before decomposition begins. For everything else (single Epic / Story / CR / Bug / Hotfix), the agent triages the request directly into the appropriate template and presents the Brief. The legacy Proposal step has been retired (CR-025 SPRINT-19); see §14.5 for the stakeholder-authored intake flow that retains the `cleargate:proposal` external label as backwards-compat.\n\n---\n\n## 3.     actual: '# ClearGate Enforcement\n\nHook-enforced rules surfaced by CLI errors. AI agents read this file when a hook trips, not at session start. Source split from `cleargate-protocol.md` per EPIC-024 (2026-04-30).\n\n## Index\n\n| New § | Source § | Title |\n|---|---|---|\n| §1 | protocol §15 | Worktree Lifecycle |\n| §2 | protocol §16 | User Walkthrough on Sprint Branch |\n| §3 | protocol §17 | Mid-Sprint Change Request Triage |\n| §4 | protocol §18 | Immediate Flashcard Gate |\n| §5 | protocol §19 | Execution Mode Routing (retired — see §15 Operator Emergency Levers) |\n| §6 | protocol §20 | File-Surface Contract |\n| §7 | protocol §22 | Advisory Readiness Gates on Push — CR-010 |\n| §8 | protocol §23 | Doctor Exit-Code Semantics |\n| §9 | protocol §24 | Lane Routing |\n| §10 | protocol §25 | Lifecycle Reconciliation (CR-017) |\n| §11 | protocol §26 | Decomposition Gate (CR-017) |\n| §12 | protocol §27 | Gate 4 — Sprint Close Acknowledgement (CR-019) |\n| §15 | — | Operator Emergency Levers (STORY-070-01) |\n| §16 | — | Host Enforcement Deltas — Codex (STORY-058-11) |\n| §17 | protocol §23.5 | Host Dispatch Modes (STORY-058-12) |\n\n---\n\n## 1. Worktree Lifecycle (source: protocol §15)\n\n**Always enforced** (STORY-070-01: `execution_mode` retired — single always-enforced behavior). Every story transition that would run a Developer agent MUST follow these procedures before any file edits begin. Set `CLEARGATE_ADVISORY=1` for break-glass downgrade to warnings (see §15).\n\n### §1.1 Branch hierarchy\n\nThe branch hierarchy for a sprint is:\n\n```\nmain\n└── sprint/S-XX          ← cut at sprint start; never commit directly\n    └── story/STORY-NNN-NN   ← cut when story transitions Ready → Bouncing\n```\n\n- **Sprint branch** is cut from `main` once at the start of each sprint:\n  ```bash\n  git checkout -b sprint/S-XX main\n  ```\n- **Story branch** is cut from the active sprint branch when the story enters `Bouncing` state:\n  ```bash\n  git checkout sprint/S-XX\n  git checkout -b story/STORY-NNN-NN sprint/S-XX\n  ```\n- Story branches are **never** cut from `main` directly; they always track the sprint branch as parent.\n\n### §1.2 Worktree commands\n\nPer-story working trees live under `.worktrees/` at repo root. Each story gets its own isolated filesystem view.\n\n**Create worktree (story starts bouncing):**\n```bash\ngit worktree add .worktrees/STORY-NNN-NN -b story/STORY-NNN-NN sprint/S-XX\n```\n\n**Verify worktree:**\n```bash\ngit worktree list\n# .../repo            <sha>  [sprint/S-XX]\n# .../repo/.worktrees/STORY-NNN-NN  <sha>  [story/STORY-NNN-NN]\n```\n\n**Merge story back into sprint branch (story passes QA + Architect):**\n```bash\ngit checkout sprint/S-XX\ngit merge story/STORY-NNN-NN --no-ff -m "merge(story/STORY-NNN-NN): STORY-NNN-NN <title>"\n```\n\n**Remove worktree and story branch (after successful merge):**\n```bash\ngit worktree remove .worktrees/STORY-NNN-NN\ngit branch -d story/STORY-NNN-NN\n```\n\n**Prune stale worktree refs:**\n```bash\ngit worktree prune\n```\n\nAll commands must be run from the **repo root** (not from inside `.worktrees/`), except Developer Agent file edits which happen inside the assigned worktree path.\n\n### §1.3 MCP nested-repo rule\n\n**The `mcp/` directory is a nested independent git repository.** Running `git worktree add` inside `mcp/` would create a worktree scoped to the nested repo, not to the outer ClearGate repo. This is a git footgun: the outer repo cannot track, merge, or remove the inner worktree via its own git commands.\n\n**Rule:** Never run `git worktree add` inside `mcp/`. If a story requires edits to `mcp/` — the nested repo has ZERO tracked files in the outer repo, so a worktree materializes no such directory at all — edit it in the main checkout. MCP-native worktree support is deferred to Q3.\n\n### §1.4 Local state.json is in-flight authority\n\nDuring a story\'s execution, `state.json` at `.cleargate/sprint-runs/<sprint-id>/state.json` is the single source of truth for story state. The MCP server is a **post-facto audit** channel: it receives state updates after each transition but is never consulted during execution. If MCP is unavailable, execution continues uninterrupted; state.json records the ground truth that MCP will eventually replicate. (Source: EPIC-013 Q7 resolution.)\n\n### §1.5 Enforcement gates\n\n**Always enforced.** Attempting to run a Developer Agent on a story without a matching `.worktrees/STORY-NNN-NN/` path present causes `validate_bounce_readiness.mjs` to exit non-zero and the orchestrator to halt the story transition. Set `CLEARGATE_ADVISORY=1` (§15) for break-glass downgrade.\n\n### §1.6 Wave worktree contract (source: protocol §23)\n\nA wave runs multiple per-story segments concurrently, each in its OWN ClearGate-managed `.worktrees/STORY-X` (created via `git worktree add .worktrees/STORY-X -b story/STORY-X sprint/S-NN` — **not** the Workflow tool\'s `isolation:\'worktree\'`, which cuts off the wrong base; spike decision 2). Two file-surface axes:\n\n- **Per-worktree `.git` index (no race).** Each segment commits inside its own worktree with an isolated `.git` index, so the pre-commit surface gate (`file_surface_diff.sh`) runs per-worktree and parallel in-segment commits do **not** race each other. File-disjointness between co-waved stories is the Architect\'s `waves.json` guarantee (the real-collision predicate, CR-124 — only ordered journals, single-writer generated artifacts and shared files needing ordered edits serialize), not a runtime lock.\n- **Shared sprint-branch axis (serial barrier merge).** `sprint/S-NN` is a single-writer axis. The barrier therefore merges each GREEN `story/STORY-X` to `sprint/S-NN` **serially — one worktree at a time, never two concurrently** (protocol §23.3). Concurrent `git worktree add` at wave launch is avoided by pre-creating all wave worktrees serially before any segment runs.\n\n---\n\n## 2. User Walkthrough on Sprint Branch (source: protocol §16)\n\n**Always enforced** (STORY-070-01): the sprint branch MUST NOT merge to `main` until the walkthrough is complete and all `UR:bug` items are resolved.\n\n> **CR-107.** When `vcs.sprint_pr: true` in `.cleargate/config.yml`, this gate gains an external artefact: a GitHub pull request opened against `main` at the walkthrough trigger (§2.1) and merged at Gate 4 close (`SKILL.md` §E.5), instead of a purely local sprint→main merge. Default `vcs.sprint_pr: false` leaves this section\'s enforcement exactly as it reads above — a local `git merge`, gated by the same MUST-NOT rule.\n\n### §2.1 Walkthrough trigger\n\nAfter all stories in the sprint are merged into `sprint/S-XX` (every story state ∈ `TERMINAL_STATES`) and before `sprint/S-XX` merges to `main`, the orchestrator invites the user to test the running application on the sprint branch.\n\n### §2.2 Feedback classification\n\nUser feedback during the walkthrough is classified into exactly two event types:\n\n| Event type | Definition | Bug-Fix Tax effect |\n|---|---|---|\n| `UR:review-feedback` | Enhancement, polish, copy change, or UX preference — does NOT fix broken behavior | Does NOT increment Bug-Fix Tax |\n| `UR:bug` | Defect, crash, wrong output, or behavior broken relative to spec | DOES increment Bug-Fix Tax |\n\n**Classification rule:** when in doubt, ask the user one targeted question — "Is this broken relative to spec, or a preference?" Do not default to `UR:bug`.\n\n### §2.3 Logging\n\nEach piece of walkthrough feedback MUST be logged in the sprint markdown file under `## 4. Mid-Sprint Change Request Triage (source: protocol §17)\n\n**Always enforced** (STORY-070-01): every user-injected change during a bounce MUST be classified before routing.\n\n### §3.1 Classification table\n\nWhen the user injects new input during a QA bounce or active story execution, the orchestrator classifies the input into one of four categories:\n\n| Event type | Definition | Bounce-counter effect | Routing |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| `CR:bug` | Defect introduced by the current story\'s implementation | Counts toward Bug-Fix Tax; increments `qa_bounces` | Re-open story; Developer fixes; QA re-verifies |\n| `CR:spec-clarification` | Clarification of existing spec — no new scope, removes ambiguity | Does NOT increment any bounce counter | Update story acceptance criteria in place; re-run impacted test |\n| `CR:scope-change` | Net-new requirement or expansion of story scope | Deferred: create a new Story in `pending-sync/`; current story continues unchanged | New Story ID assigned; current bounce counter unaffected |\n| `CR:approach-change` | Switch implementation approach without changing functional spec | Does NOT increment bounce counter; resets Developer context | Re-spawn Developer with updated approach note; same story ID |\n\n### §3.2 Logging\n\nEach mid-sprint CR MUST be logged in the sprint markdown file under `## 4. Source split from `cleargate-protocol.md` per EPIC-024 (2026-04-30).\n\n## Index\n\n| New § | Source § | Title |\n|---|---|---|\n| §1 | protocol §15 | Worktree Lifecycle |\n| §2 | protocol §16 | User Walkthrough on Sprint Branch |\n| §3 | protocol §17 | Mid-Sprint Change Request Triage |\n| §4 | protocol §18 | Immediate Flashcard Gate |\n| §5 | protocol §19 | Execution Mode Routing (retired — see §15 Operator Emergency Levers) |\n| §6 | protocol §20 | File-Surface Contract |\n| §7 | protocol §22 | Advisory Readiness Gates on Push — CR-010 |\n| §8 | protocol §23 | Doctor Exit-Code Semantics |\n| §9 | protocol §24 | Lane Routing |\n| §10 | protocol §25 | Lifecycle Reconciliation (CR-017) |\n| §11 | protocol §26 | Decomposition Gate (CR-017) |\n| §12 | protocol §27 | Gate 4 — Sprint Close Acknowledgement (CR-019) |\n| §15 | — | Operator Emergency Levers (STORY-070-01) |\n| §16 | — | Host Enforcement Deltas — Codex (STORY-058-11) |\n| §17 | protocol §23.5 | Host Dispatch Modes (STORY-058-12) |\n\n---\n\n## 1. Worktree Lifecycle (source: protocol §15)\n\n**Always enforced** (STORY-070-01: `execution_mode` retired — single always-enforced behavior). Every story transition that would run a Developer agent MUST follow these procedures before any file edits begin. Set `CLEARGATE_ADVISORY=1` for break-glass downgrade to warnings (see §15).\n\n### §1.1 Branch hierarchy\n\nThe branch hierarchy for a sprint is:\n\n```\nmain\n└── sprint/S-XX          ← cut at sprint start; never commit directly\n    └── story/STORY-NNN-NN   ← cut when story transitions Ready → Bouncing\n```\n\n- **Sprint branch** is cut from `main` once at the start of each sprint:\n  ```bash\n  git checkout -b sprint/S-XX main\n  ```\n- **Story branch** is cut from the active sprint branch when the story enters `Bouncing` state:\n  ```bash\n  git checkout sprint/S-XX\n  git checkout -b story/STORY-NNN-NN sprint/S-XX\n  ```\n- Story branches are **never** cut from `main` directly; they always track the sprint branch as parent.\n\n### §1.2 Worktree commands\n\nPer-story working trees live under `.worktrees/` at repo root. Each story gets its own isolated filesystem view.\n\n**Create worktree (story starts bouncing):**\n```bash\ngit worktree add .worktrees/STORY-NNN-NN -b story/STORY-NNN-NN sprint/S-XX\n```\n\n**Verify worktree:**\n```bash\ngit worktree list\n# .../repo            <sha>  [sprint/S-XX]\n# .../repo/.worktrees/STORY-NNN-NN  <sha>  [story/STORY-NNN-NN]\n```\n\n**Merge story back into sprint branch (story passes QA + Architect):**\n```bash\ngit checkout sprint/S-XX\ngit merge story/STORY-NNN-NN --no-ff -m "merge(story/STORY-NNN-NN): STORY-NNN-NN <title>"\n```\n\n**Remove worktree and story branch (after successful merge):**\n```bash\ngit worktree remove .worktrees/STORY-NNN-NN\ngit branch -d story/STORY-NNN-NN\n```\n\n**Prune stale worktree refs:**\n```bash\ngit worktree prune\n```\n\nAll commands must be run from the **repo root** (not from inside `.worktrees/`), except Developer Agent file edits which happen inside the assigned worktree path.\n\n### §1.3 MCP nested-repo rule\n\n**The `mcp/` directory is a nested independent git repository.** Running `git worktree add` inside `mcp/` would create a worktree scoped to the nested repo, not to the outer ClearGate repo. This is a git footgun: the outer repo cannot track, merge, or remove the inner worktree via its own git commands.\n\n**Rule:** Never run `git worktree add` inside `mcp/`. If a story requires edits to `mcp/` — the nested repo has ZERO tracked files in the outer repo, so a worktree materializes no such directory at all — edit it in the main checkout. MCP-native worktree support is deferred to Q3.\n\n### §1.4 Local state.json is in-flight authority\n\nDuring a story\'s execution, `state.json` at `.cleargate/sprint-runs/<sprint-id>/state.json` is the single source of truth for story state. The MCP server is a **post-facto audit** channel: it receives state updates after each transition but is never consulted during execution. If MCP is unavailable, execution continues uninterrupted; state.json records the ground truth that MCP will eventually replicate. (Source: EPIC-013 Q7 resolution.)\n\n### §1.5 Enforcement gates\n\n**Always enforced.** Attempting to run a Developer Agent on a story without a matching `.worktrees/STORY-NNN-NN/` path present causes `validate_bounce_readiness.mjs` to exit non-zero and the orchestrator to halt the story transition. Set `CLEARGATE_ADVISORY=1` (§15) for break-glass downgrade.\n\n### §1.6 Wave worktree contract (source: protocol §23)\n\nA wave runs multiple per-story segments concurrently, each in its OWN ClearGate-managed `.worktrees/STORY-X` (created via `git worktree add .worktrees/STORY-X -b story/STORY-X sprint/S-NN` — **not** the Workflow tool\'s `isolation:\'worktree\'`, which cuts off the wrong base; spike decision 2). Two file-surface axes:\n\n- **Per-worktree `.git` index (no race).** Each segment commits inside its own worktree with an isolated `.git` index, so the pre-commit surface gate (`file_surface_diff.sh`) runs per-worktree and parallel in-segment commits do **not** race each other. File-disjointness between co-waved stories is the Architect\'s `waves.json` guarantee (the real-collision predicate, CR-124 — only ordered journals, single-writer generated artifacts and shared files needing ordered edits serialize), not a runtime lock.\n- **Shared sprint-branch axis (serial barrier merge).** `sprint/S-NN` is a single-writer axis. The barrier therefore merges each GREEN `story/STORY-X` to `sprint/S-NN` **serially — one worktree at a time, never two concurrently** (protocol §23.3). Concurrent `git worktree add` at wave launch is avoided by pre-creating all wave worktrees serially before any segment runs.\n\n---\n\n## 2.
